```py
plain fread `FUN_0049de96`, then the rest is encrypted. `Sound.dat` is encrypted
from offset 0. `Data/Shape.dat`, `Skeleton.dat`, `Image*.dat` are NOT encrypted.

    python -I bfd_crypt.py <in> <out> [--full]     # --full: decrypt from offset 0
"""
import sys

XOR = 0x59


def dec_byte(x):
    x = (x ^ XOR) & 0xFF
    return ((x << 1) | (x >> 7)) & 0xFF


def decrypt(buf, skip_prefix=4):
    """Return plaintext: first `skip_prefix` bytes verbatim, rest decrypted."""
    head = bytes(buf[:skip_prefix])
    return head + bytes(dec_byte(x) for x in buf[skip_prefix:])


def main():
    args = [a for a in sys.argv[1:] if not a.startswith("-")]
    if len(args) != 2:
        print(__doc__)
        return 1
    skip = 0 if "--full" in sys.argv else 4
    data = open(args[0], "rb").read()
    out = decrypt(data, skip)
    open(args[1], "wb").write(out)
    print(f"decrypted {len(data)} bytes (skip_prefix={skip}) -> {args[1]}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/build_catalog.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Scan an installed AXIS game directory and emit web/catalog.json describing
its archives (entry names, sizes, and PTX texture metadata) plus loose media.

Only metadata is written — never pixel/mesh bytes — so the catalog is small and
the copyrighted payloads stay on disk. See docs/SPEC.md for the formats.

    python -I build_catalog.py <game_dir> <out_json>
"""
import sys
import os
import json
import struct
import re

REC, NAME = 0x44, 0x40

# encrypted data files (see docs/SPEC.md §2.3): rol8(b ^ 0x59, 1) after fread.
# Sound.dat is encrypted from offset 0; the others keep a 4-byte plaintext version.
ENCRYPTED = {"item.dat": 4, "component.dat": 4, "sound.dat": 0}


def _dec_byte(x):
    x = (x ^ 0x59) & 0xFF
    return ((x << 1) | (x >> 7)) & 0xFF


def decrypt(buf, skip):
    return bytes(buf[:skip]) + bytes(_dec_byte(x) for x in buf[skip:])


def read_encrypted(path, skip):
    """Decrypt an obfuscated .dat and return its version, header count, and the
    distinct asset path names embedded in it (record layout varies per file)."""
    raw = open(path, "rb").read()
    plain = decrypt(raw, skip)
    ver = struct.unpack_from("<I", plain, 0)[0] if skip == 4 else None
    count = struct.unpack_from("<I", plain, 4)[0] if (skip == 4 and len(plain) >= 8) else None
    names, seen = [], set()
    for m in re.finditer(rb"[\x20-\x7e]{4,}", plain):
        s = m.group().decode("cp949", "replace").strip()
        if ("\\" in s or "/" in s) and s not in seen:
            seen.add(s)
            names.append(s)
    return {"indexed": True, "encrypted": True, "version": hex(ver) if ver else None,
            "header_count": count, "file_size": len(raw),
            "count": len(names), "entries": [{"name": n} for n in names]}


def read_toc(path):
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        blob = f.read()
    if len(blob) < 4:
        return None
    (count,) = struct.unpack_from("<I", blob, 0)
    toc_end = 4 + count * REC
    # plausibility: TOC must fit and first offset must equal toc_end
    if count <= 0 or toc_end > len(blob):
        return None
    entries = []
    for i in range(count):
        base = 4 + i * REC
        name = blob[base:base + NAME].split(b"\x00")[0].decode("cp949", "replace")
        (off,) = struct.unpack_from("<I", blob, base + NAME)
        entries.append((name, off))
    if not entries or entries[0][1] != toc_end:
        return None  # not this archive shape (e.g. the obfuscated blobs)
    out = []
    for i, (name, off) in enumerate(entries):
        end = entries[i + 1][1] if i + 1 < len(entries) else len(blob)
        rec = {"name": name, "size": end - off}
        if end - off >= 16 and blob[off:off + 4] == b"PTX\x00":
            ver, w8, h8, fmt, table, _ = struct.unpack_from("<HHHHHH", blob, off + 4)
            rec["ptx"] = {"w": w8 * 8, "h": h8 * 8,
                          "fmt": {0x81: "RGB", 0x82: "RGBA"}.get(fmt, hex(fmt)),
                          "table": table}
        out.append(rec)
    return {"count": count, "entries": out}


def list_dir(game, sub, exts):
    d = os.path.join(game, sub)
    if not os.path.isdir(d):
        return []
    items = []
    for fn in sorted(os.listdir(d)):
        if os.path.splitext(fn)[1].lower() in exts:
            items.append({"name": fn, "size": os.path.getsize(os.path.join(d, fn))})
    return items


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) != 3:
        print(__doc__)
        return 1
    game, out_json = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    cat = {"game_dir": os.path.abspath(game), "archives": {}, "media": {}}

    data_dir = os.path.join(game, "Data")
    if os.path.isdir(data_dir):
        for fn in sorted(os.listdir(data_dir)):
            if not fn.lower().endswith(".dat"):
                continue
            full = os.path.join(data_dir, fn)
            size = os.path.getsize(full)
            if fn.lower() in ENCRYPTED:
                try:
                    cat["archives"][fn] = read_encrypted(full, ENCRYPTED[fn.lower()])
                except Exception as e:
                    cat["archives"][fn] = {"indexed": False, "file_size": size,
                                           "note": f"decrypt failed: {e}"}
                continue
            toc = read_toc(full)
            if toc:
                cat["archives"][fn] = {"indexed": True, "file_size": size, **toc}
            else:
                cat["archives"][fn] = {"indexed": False, "file_size": size,
                                       "note": "binary blob (loader not yet reversed)"}

    cat["media"]["maps"] = list_dir(game, "Map", {".bfd"})
    # tag each map with its container version (0x1201 expected)
    map_dir = os.path.join(game, "Map")
    for m in cat["media"]["maps"]:
        try:
            with open(os.path.join(map_dir, m["name"]), "rb") as f:
                (ver,) = struct.unpack("<I", f.read(4))
            m["version"] = f"0x{ver:x}"
        except Exception:
            pass
    cat["media"]["music"] = list_dir(game, "Music", {".mp3"})
    cat["media"]["sound"] = list_dir(game, "Sound", {".wav"})

    with open(out_json, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(cat, f, ensure_ascii=False)
    n_arch = len(cat["archives"])
    n_tex = sum(sum(1 for e in a.get("entries", []) if "ptx" in e)
                for a in cat["archives"].values())
    print(f"catalog: {n_arch} archives, {n_tex} PTX textures, "
          f"{len(cat['media']['maps'])} maps, {len(cat['media']['music'])} music "
          f"-> {out_json}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/export_shapes.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Export AXIS Shape.dat meshes to per-shape JSON for the web mesh viewer.

Exports every shape whose .shp blob parses with full byte-consumption (currently
the 948 single-group meshes; multi-group inter-group padding is still being
pinned). See docs/SPEC.md §2.7. Output is metadata derived from game data — keep
it out of the repository.

    python -I export_shapes.py <Shape.dat> <out_dir> [--limit N]
"""
import sys
import os
import json
import struct

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import parse_shp

REC, NAME = 0x44, 0x40


def clean(vecs):
    return [[0.0 if (x != x or abs(x) > 1e9) else round(x, 3) for x in p] for p in vecs]


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) < 3:
        print(__doc__)
        return 1
    path, out_dir = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    limit = int(sys.argv[sys.argv.index("--limit") + 1]) if "--limit" in sys.argv else None
    os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)
    raw = open(path, "rb").read()
    count = struct.unpack_from("<I", raw, 0)[0]
    ents = []
    for i in range(count):
        b = 4 + i * REC
        name = raw[b:b + NAME].split(b"\x00")[0].decode("cp949", "replace")
        off = struct.unpack_from("<I", raw, b + NAME)[0]
        ents.append((name, off))

    index, done = [], 0
    for i, (name, off) in enumerate(ents):
        end = ents[i + 1][1] if i + 1 < len(ents) else len(raw)
        blob = raw[off:end]
        try:
            r = parse_shp.parse(blob)
        except Exception:
            continue
        if r["consumed"] != len(blob):
            continue  # only fully byte-validated meshes
        # merge all groups into one mesh, offsetting indices per group
        pos, nrm, uv, tris = [], [], [], []
        for g in r["groups"]:
            base = len(pos)
            pos += g["pos"]; nrm += g["nrm"]; uv += g["uv"]
            tris += [(a + base, b + base, c + base) for (a, b, c) in g["tris"]]
        if not pos or not tris:
            continue
        safe = name.replace("\\", "_").replace("/", "_")
        with open(os.path.join(out_dir, safe + ".json"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            json.dump({"name": name, "pos": clean(pos), "nrm": clean(nrm),
                       "uv": clean(uv), "tris": tris}, f, allow_nan=False)
        index.append({"id": safe, "name": name, "v": len(pos), "t": len(tris),
                      "g": len(r["groups"])})
        done += 1
        if limit and done >= limit:
            break
    index.sort(key=lambda e: e["name"])
    with open(os.path.join(out_dir, "index.json"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(index, f, ensure_ascii=False)
    print(f"exported {done} meshes -> {out_dir}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/export_textures.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Decode AXIS textures (Image*.dat PTX entries) to PNG for the web gallery.

Uses ptx_decode (baseline-JPEG). Output is derived from copyrighted game data —
keep it out of the repository.

    python -I export_textures.py <game_dir> <out_dir> [--limit N]
"""
import sys
import os
import json
import struct

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import ptx_decode

REC, NAME = 0x44, 0x40


def main():
    args = [a for a in sys.argv[1:] if not a.startswith("--")]
    if len(args) < 2:
        print(__doc__)
        return 1
    game, out_dir = args[0], args[1]
    limit = int(sys.argv[sys.argv.index("--limit") + 1]) if "--limit" in sys.argv else None
    os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)
    exe = open(os.path.join(game, "AXIS.EXE"), "rb").read()
    data_dir = os.path.join(game, "Data")

    index, done = [], 0
    for fn in sorted(os.listdir(data_dir)):
        if not (fn.lower().startswith("image") and fn.lower().endswith(".dat")):
            continue
        raw = open(os.path.join(data_dir, fn), "rb").read()
        count = struct.unpack_from("<I", raw, 0)[0]
        ents = []
        for i in range(count):
            b = 4 + i * REC
            name = raw[b:b + NAME].split(b"\x00")[0].decode("cp949", "replace")
            off = struct.unpack_from("<I", raw, b + NAME)[0]
            ents.append((name, off))
        for i, (name, off) in enumerate(ents):
            end = ents[i + 1][1] if i + 1 < len(ents) else len(raw)
            blob = raw[off:end]
            if blob[:4] != b"PTX\x00":
                continue
            try:
                w, h, rgba = ptx_decode.decode(blob, exe)
            except Exception:
                continue
            safe = (fn[:-4] + "_" + name).replace("\\", "_").replace("/", "_")
            ptx_decode.write_png(os.path.join(out_dir, safe + ".png"), w, h, rgba)
            index.append({"id": safe, "name": name, "src": fn, "w": w, "h": h})
            done += 1
            if limit and done >= limit:
                break
        if limit and done >= limit:
            break
    index.sort(key=lambda e: e["name"])
    with open(os.path.join(out_dir, "index.json"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(index, f, ensure_ascii=False)
    print(f"decoded {done} textures -> {out_dir}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/extract_archive.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Extract every entry from an AXIS indexed .dat archive to a directory, and
summarise PTX texture headers.

Archive format and PTX header: see docs/SPEC.md (derived from AXIS.EXE
FUN_004660a0 / FUN_00454510 / FUN_0046df80).

Run isolated:
    python -I extract_archive.py <file.dat> [out_dir] [--manifest manifest.json]

With no out_dir it only prints a summary + writes the manifest (no files dumped).
Never commit extracted output — it is derived from copyrighted game data.
"""
import sys
import os
import json
import struct

REC = 0x44
NAME = 0x40


def read_toc(blob):
    (count,) = struct.unpack_from("<I", blob, 0)
    entries = []
    for i in range(count):
        base = 4 + i * REC
        name = blob[base:base + NAME].split(b"\x00")[0].decode("cp949", "replace")
        (off,) = struct.unpack_from("<I", blob, base + NAME)
        entries.append((name, off))
    out = []
    for i, (name, off) in enumerate(entries):
        end = entries[i + 1][1] if i + 1 < len(entries) else len(blob)
        out.append((name, off, end - off))
    return count, out


def parse_ptx(data):
    """Return dict of PTX header fields, or None if not a PTX blob."""
    if len(data) < 16 or data[:4] != b"PTX\x00":
        return None
    ver, w8, h8, fmt, table, _pad = struct.unpack_from("<HHHHHH", data, 4)
    return {
        "version": ver,
        "width": w8 * 8,
        "height": h8 * 8,
        "format": "RGBA" if fmt == 0x82 else "RGB" if fmt == 0x81 else f"0x{fmt:x}",
        "planes": 4 if fmt == 0x82 else 3 if fmt == 0x81 else None,
        "table": table,
    }


def safe_rel(name):
    # archive paths use backslashes; keep them as sub-dirs, strip traversal
    parts = [p for p in name.replace("\\", "/").split("/") if p not in ("", ".", "..")]
    return os.path.join(*parts) if parts else "entry"


def main():
    args = [a for a in sys.argv[1:] if not a.startswith("--")]
    manifest_path = None
    if "--manifest" in sys.argv:
        manifest_path = sys.argv[sys.argv.index("--manifest") + 1]
    if not args:
        print(__doc__)
        return 1
    path = args[0]
    out_dir = args[1] if len(args) > 1 else None

    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        blob = f.read()
    count, entries = read_toc(blob)

    manifest = {"file": os.path.basename(path), "count": count, "entries": []}
    ptx_count = 0
    for name, off, size in entries:
        rec = {"name": name, "offset": off, "size": size}
        ptx = parse_ptx(blob[off:off + 16])
        if ptx:
            ptx_count += 1
            rec["ptx"] = ptx
        manifest["entries"].append(rec)
        if out_dir:
            dst = os.path.join(out_dir, safe_rel(name)) + ".bin"
            os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(dst), exist_ok=True)
            with open(dst, "wb") as g:
                g.write(blob[off:off + size])

    print(f"{manifest['file']}: {count} entries, {ptx_count} PTX textures"
          + (f", extracted to {out_dir}" if out_dir else " (summary only)"))
    if ptx_count:
        dims = {}
        for e in manifest["entries"]:
            if "ptx" in e:
                k = f"{e['ptx']['width']}x{e['ptx']['height']} {e['ptx']['format']}"
                dims[k] = dims.get(k, 0) + 1
        print("  PTX sizes: " + ", ".join(f"{k}:{v}" for k, v in sorted(dims.items())))
    if manifest_path:
        with open(manifest_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as g:
            json.dump(manifest, g, ensure_ascii=False, indent=0)
        print(f"  manifest -> {manifest_path}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/extract_map.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Decrypt an AXIS .bfd map and extract its object-placement list to JSON.

Layout (decrypted; see docs/SPEC.md §2.3/§2.6):
    u32  version (0x1201)
    u32  section_size  (== count*0xB0 + 4)
    u32  count
    count × 0xB0 (176-byte) object records:
        +0x00  char   shape path name (NUL-terminated, cp949)
        +0x8c  f32[3] position (x, y, z)   [confirmed by spatial spread]
        (other floats: orientation/scale — not yet identified)

The object meshes themselves live in Shape.dat (mesh format still to be decoded),
so this is the map's spatial *layout*, not yet its rendered geometry.

    python -I extract_map.py <map.bfd> <out.json>
"""
import sys
import os
import struct

REC = 0xB0
NAME = 0x88
POS = 0x8C


def _dec(x):
    x = (x ^ 0x59) & 0xFF
    return ((x << 1) | (x >> 7)) & 0xFF


def decrypt(buf):
    return bytes(buf[:4]) + bytes(_dec(x) for x in buf[4:])


def extract(path):
    plain = decrypt(open(path, "rb").read())
    ver = struct.unpack_from("<I", plain, 0)[0]
    section = struct.unpack_from("<I", plain, 4)[0]
    count = struct.unpack_from("<I", plain, 8)[0]
    layout_ok = (section == count * REC + 4)
    objs = []
    base = 0xC
    for i in range(count):
        o = base + i * REC
        if o + REC > len(plain):
            break
        name = plain[o:o + NAME].split(b"\x00")[0].decode("cp949", "replace")
        x, y, z = struct.unpack_from("<fff", plain, o + POS)
        if any(v != v or abs(v) > 1e7 for v in (x, y, z)):
            x = y = z = 0.0
        objs.append({"name": name, "pos": [round(x, 2), round(y, 2), round(z, 2)]})
    return {"map": os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(path))[0],
            "version": hex(ver), "count": count, "layout_ok": layout_ok,
            "objects": objs}


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) != 3:
        print(__doc__)
        return 1
    import json
    data = extract(sys.argv[1])
    with open(sys.argv[2], "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(data, f, ensure_ascii=False)
    print(f"{data['map']}: {len(data['objects'])} objects, "
          f"layout_ok={data['layout_ok']} -> {sys.argv[2]}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/parse_ape.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Decode an AXIS AutoPlay replay (.ape) into per-actor movement + events.

A `.ape` is an encrypted (§2.3) stream of 76-byte records written by the replay
logger `FUN_0046c0b0`:  `[u32 tick][u32 arg][u32 0x40][64-byte payload]` — the
payload is exactly the 64-byte network packet the game sends (SPEC §4). Packet
type is `payload[1]`; the three builders decoded so far:

  TYPE 0  (builder FUN_0048d750) -- per-tick STATE SNAPSHOT, ~96% of traffic.
      [1]=0
      [4]      packed = (actor<<4) | mode/flags     -> actor = [4] >> 4
      [5],[7],[9]  int16  quantized WORLD position x,y,z  (float->int via __ftol)
      [0xb..0x12]  orientation / velocity / weapon-fire flag bytes
      [0x13..0x3f] five 9-byte weapon/turret sub-records
                   (flags + target id + 3 aim shorts each)
      -> VERIFIED: consecutive samples form smooth world-scale trajectories.
  TYPE 1  (FUN_0048d080) -- player INPUT/action event: normalised input axes
      ([17]/[21] f32, 0..1), action/fire bitmasks ([25]/[29] u16), and an RNG
      sync block ([43..55], shared table) for hit/damage determinism.
  TYPE 2  (FUN_0048d580) -- player IDENTITY/loadout: name string ([2..18]),
      actor ([22]), ~11 equipment ids ([23..33], +1 encoded), 20-byte stat block.
  TYPE 12 -- session/control (rare).

So movement IS replicated (type 0 positions), with input+RNG events (type 1)
layered on top — a state-replication model, not pure lockstep.

    python -I parse_ape.py <file.ape> [out.json]
"""
import sys
import struct

REC = 76
PAY = 12
POS_SCALE = 10           # type-0 coords are int16; apply side (FUN_0048bca0) does *10.0


def _dec(b):
    def d(x):
        x = (x ^ 0x59) & 0xFF
        return ((x << 1) | (x >> 7)) & 0xFF
    return bytes(d(x) for x in b)


def decrypt(raw):
    p = _dec(raw)
    if struct.unpack_from("<I", p, 0)[0] == 0x1201:
        return p
    return bytes(raw[:4]) + _dec(raw[4:])


def find_start(plain):
    for o in range(0, min(len(plain), 4096)):
        if all(o + k * REC + REC <= len(plain) and
               struct.unpack_from("<I", plain, o + k * REC + 8)[0] == 0x40
               for k in range(3)):
            return o
    return None


def parse(raw):
    plain = decrypt(raw)
    start = find_start(plain)
    if start is None:
        return {"records": 0, "types": {}, "tracks": {}, "names": {}}
    types, tracks, names = {}, {}, {}
    nrec = 0
    o = start
    while o + REC <= len(plain):
        if struct.unpack_from("<I", plain, o + 8)[0] != 0x40:
            break
        tick = struct.unpack_from("<I", plain, o)[0]
        pay = plain[o + PAY:o + PAY + 64]
        nrec += 1
        t = pay[1]
        types[t] = types.get(t, 0) + 1
        if t == 0:                                   # state snapshot -> trajectory
            actor = pay[4] >> 4
            x, y, z = struct.unpack_from("<hhh", pay, 5)
            yaw = pay[0x0c] * 14.0625           # heading, X-Z plane (apply side)
            if yaw > 1800:
                yaw -= 3600
            tracks.setdefault(actor, []).append(
                [tick, x * POS_SCALE, y * POS_SCALE, z * POS_SCALE, round(yaw / 10, 1)])
        elif t == 2:                                 # identity -> name
            raw_name = pay[2:19].split(b"\x00")[0]
            try:
                nm = raw_name.decode("cp949", "replace")
            except Exception:
                nm = ""
            if nm:
                names[pay[22] if pay[22] < 16 else 0] = nm
        o += REC
    return {"records": nrec, "start": start, "types": types,
            "tracks": tracks, "names": names}


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) < 2:
        print(__doc__)
        return 1
    raw = open(sys.argv[1], "rb").read()
    r = parse(raw)
    print(f"records={r['records']} start={r.get('start')}")
    print(f"packet types (payload[1]): {dict(sorted(r['types'].items()))}  "
          f"[0=state/pos 1=input 2=identity 12=session]")
    if r["names"]:
        print(f"names: {r['names']}")
    for a in sorted(r["tracks"]):
        pts = r["tracks"][a]
        xs = [p[1] for p in pts]
        ys = [p[2] for p in pts]
        zs = [p[3] for p in pts]
        print(f"  actor {a}: {len(pts):4d} positions  "
              f"x[{min(xs):6d},{max(xs):6d}] y[{min(ys):6d},{max(ys):6d}] z[{min(zs):6d},{max(zs):6d}]")
    if len(sys.argv) > 2:
        import json
        json.dump(r, open(sys.argv[2], "w"))
        print(f"-> {sys.argv[2]}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/parse_archive.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""AXIS .dat archive index parser.

Format (confirmed from AXIS.EXE FUN_004660a0, the Shape.dat loader):
    [u32 count]
    count * record, each 0x44 (68) bytes:
        char name[0x40]   # NUL-terminated path, may hold extra NUL-separated labels
        u32  data_offset  # offset into the file where this entry's data begins
    <data blob follows the TOC>

Entry data size is inferred as next_offset - this_offset (last entry runs to EOF).

Run isolated (untrusted game data is passed as an argument, not imported):
    python -I parse_archive.py <path-to.dat>
"""
import sys
import struct

REC = 0x44        # 68-byte record
NAME = 0x40       # 64-byte name field


def parse(path):
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        blob = f.read()
    (count,) = struct.unpack_from("<I", blob, 0)
    toc_end = 4 + count * REC
    entries = []
    for i in range(count):
        base = 4 + i * REC
        raw = blob[base:base + NAME]
        # primary name = bytes up to first NUL; collect extra NUL-separated labels too
        parts = [p.decode("cp949", "replace") for p in raw.split(b"\x00") if p]
        (offset,) = struct.unpack_from("<I", blob, base + NAME)
        entries.append((parts[0] if parts else "", offset, parts[1:]))

    # infer sizes from successive offsets
    sized = []
    for i, (name, off, extra) in enumerate(entries):
        end = entries[i + 1][1] if i + 1 < len(entries) else len(blob)
        sized.append((name, off, end - off, extra))
    return count, toc_end, len(blob), sized


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) != 2:
        print("usage: python -I parse_archive.py <file.dat>")
        return 1
    path = sys.argv[1]
    count, toc_end, filesize, sized = parse(path)
    print(f"file        : {path}")
    print(f"entries     : {count}")
    print(f"TOC size    : {toc_end} bytes (4 + {count}*{REC})")
    print(f"file size   : {filesize} bytes")
    first_off = sized[0][1] if sized else 0
    print(f"1st data off: {first_off}  (TOC_end={toc_end}, match={first_off == toc_end})")
    # sanity: offsets monotonic non-decreasing and within file?
    offs = [o for _, o, _, _ in sized]
    mono = all(offs[i] <= offs[i + 1] for i in range(len(offs) - 1))
    in_range = all(toc_end <= o <= filesize for o in offs)
    print(f"offsets     : monotonic={mono} in_range={in_range}")
    print("\n-- first 15 entries (name | offset | size | extra-labels) --")
    for name, off, size, extra in sized[:15]:
        ex = (" " + " ".join(extra)) if extra else ""
        print(f"  {name:<28} @{off:>9} {size:>8}B{ex}")
    print("\n-- 5 largest entries --")
    for name, off, size, extra in sorted(sized, key=lambda e: -e[2])[:5]:
        print(f"  {name:<28} {size:>10}B")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/parse_bfd.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Decrypt an AXIS .bfd map (or any file using the same codec) and inspect it:
print the version prefix and the embedded ASCII name strings (shape/object paths)
with their offsets. Full terrain parsing is WIP — see docs/SPEC.md §2.5; the
field layout is still being pinned against decrypted bytes.

    python -I parse_bfd.py <map.bfd> [--full] [--save out.bin]
"""
import sys
import os
import struct
import re

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
from bfd_crypt import decrypt


def ascii_runs(buf, minlen=4):
    for m in re.finditer(rb"[\x20-\x7e]{%d,}" % minlen, buf):
        yield m.start(), m.group().decode("ascii", "replace")


def main():
    args = [a for a in sys.argv[1:] if not a.startswith("-")]
    if not args:
        print(__doc__)
        return 1
    skip = 0 if "--full" in sys.argv else 4
    data = open(args[0], "rb").read()
    plain = decrypt(data, skip)

    print(f"file   : {args[0]}  ({len(data)} bytes)")
    if skip == 4:
        print(f"version: {struct.unpack_from('<I', plain, 0)[0]:#x}")
    print(f"u32[1] : {struct.unpack_from('<I', plain, 4)[0]}")
    print(f"u32[2] : {struct.unpack_from('<I', plain, 8)[0]}")

    runs = [(o, s) for o, s in ascii_runs(plain, 5)]
    print(f"\nembedded names ({len(runs)}):")
    for o, s in runs[:60]:
        print(f"  @{o:#07x}  {s}")
    if len(runs) > 60:
        print(f"  … +{len(runs) - 60} more")

    if "--save" in sys.argv:
        out = sys.argv[sys.argv.index("--save") + 1]
        open(out, "wb").write(plain)
        print(f"\ndecrypted -> {out}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/parse_mathfunc.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Parse MATHFUNC.TBL — AXIS's math lookup tables + the shared RNG pool.

Loaded at startup by AXIS.EXE FUN_0046b6f0 via plain fread (the file is NOT
encrypted). Layout (confirmed: file is exactly 59584 = (3600*3 + 4096)*4 bytes):

    float32 sin[3600]   # DAT_00a1cc48 : sin(i * 0.1 deg)
    float32 cos[3600]   # DAT_00a19408 : cos(i * 0.1 deg)
    float32 tan[3600]   # DAT_00a15bc8 : tan(i * 0.1 deg)
    uint32  rng[4096]   # DAT_00a11bc8 : the shared RNG pool

The RNG pool is the backbone of AXIS's lockstep determinism (SPEC §5): every
peer loads the same pool and consumes it by a single shared index
`pool[idx++ & 0xfff] % N`, so spawns / AI / damage rolls match across clients.
The pool is a precomputed table of standard C `rand()` outputs (0..32767) —
its first entries are the canonical rand() sequence 41,18467,6334,26500,…

This parser VERIFIES the decode: the sin/cos tables must match the real math
functions (to float precision), which proves the layout — and therefore that
the RNG pool is extracted from the correct offset.

    python -I parse_mathfunc.py <MATHFUNC.TBL> [rng_out.json]
"""
import sys
import struct
import math
import json

N = 3600
NP = 4096


def parse(raw):
    sin = struct.unpack_from("<%df" % N, raw, 0)
    cos = struct.unpack_from("<%df" % N, raw, N * 4)
    tan = struct.unpack_from("<%df" % N, raw, N * 8)
    rng = struct.unpack_from("<%dI" % NP, raw, N * 12)
    return sin, cos, tan, rng


def verify(sin, cos, tan):
    es = max(abs(sin[i] - math.sin(math.radians(i * 0.1))) for i in range(N))
    ec = max(abs(cos[i] - math.cos(math.radians(i * 0.1))) for i in range(N))
    # tan diverges near 90 deg; check only where it is well-conditioned
    et = max(abs(tan[i] - math.tan(math.radians(i * 0.1)))
             for i in range(N) if abs(math.cos(math.radians(i * 0.1))) > 0.05)
    return es, ec, et


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) < 2:
        print("usage: python -I parse_mathfunc.py <MATHFUNC.TBL> [rng_out.json]")
        return 1
    raw = open(sys.argv[1], "rb").read()
    print(f"file: {sys.argv[1]}  ({len(raw)} bytes, expected {(N*3+NP)*4})")
    if len(raw) != (N * 3 + NP) * 4:
        print("!! unexpected size — layout may differ")
    sin, cos, tan, rng = parse(raw)
    es, ec, et = verify(sin, cos, tan)
    print(f"sin table: max err vs sin(i*0.1deg) = {es:.3g}   {'OK' if es < 1e-4 else 'MISMATCH'}")
    print(f"cos table: max err vs cos(i*0.1deg) = {ec:.3g}   {'OK' if ec < 1e-4 else 'MISMATCH'}")
    print(f"tan table: max err (|cos|>0.05)     = {et:.3g}   {'OK' if et < 1e-2 else 'MISMATCH'}")
    print(f"\nRNG pool: {NP} entries, range [{min(rng)},{max(rng)}]  "
          f"{'(rand() 0..32767)' if max(rng) <= 32767 else ''}")
    print(f"  first 8 : {list(rng[:8])}")
    print(f"  distinct: {len(set(rng))}/{NP}")
    if len(sys.argv) > 2:
        json.dump({"rng": list(rng)}, open(sys.argv[2], "w"))
        print(f"-> {sys.argv[2]} (RNG pool only)")
    ok = es < 1e-4 and ec < 1e-4 and et < 1e-2
    print("\nDECODE VERIFIED" if ok else "\nDECODE FAILED")
    return 0 if ok else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/parse_shp.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""AXIS .shp mesh parser (the Shape.dat blob format, tag 0x71110300).

Reverse-engineered from FUN_004655c0. Layout (plaintext; Shape.dat is NOT encrypted):

  object header  0x4C bytes:
      +0x10 i32 groupCount      +0x20 f32[3] bbox-seed     +0x30 u32 tag (0x71110300)
  groupCount × group header, 0x80 bytes each:
      +0x34 i32 vertexCount     +0x48 i32 faceGroupCount
      +0x50 f32[6] bbox (min xyz, max xyz)   (other fields are saved pointers, ignored)
  then, per group, the data blocks in order:
      positions   vertexCount × vec3 (12B)
      normals     vertexCount × vec3 (12B)
      attr        vertexCount × 4B    (colour/flags)
      uv          vertexCount × 2×f32 (8B)
      faceGroups  faceGroupCount × 12B  {u32 ?, u32 indexCount, u32 savedPtr}
      indices     per faceGroup: indexCount × u32

    python -I parse_shp.py <Shape.dat> <offset> <size> [--json out.json]
"""
import sys
import struct

OBJ_HDR = 0x4C
GRP_HDR = 0x80
TAG = 0x71110300


def parse(blob):
    (tag,) = struct.unpack_from("<I", blob, 0x30)
    (gcount,) = struct.unpack_from("<i", blob, 0x10)
    if tag != TAG:
        raise ValueError(f"unexpected tag {tag:#x} (only new format 0x71110300 handled)")
    groups_meta = []
    for gi in range(gcount):
        g = OBJ_HDR + gi * GRP_HDR
        vc = struct.unpack_from("<i", blob, g + 0x34)[0]
        fgc = struct.unpack_from("<i", blob, g + 0x48)[0]
        bbox = struct.unpack_from("<6f", blob, g + 0x50)
        groups_meta.append((vc, fgc, bbox))

    p = OBJ_HDR + gcount * GRP_HDR
    groups = []
    for vc, fgc, bbox in groups_meta:
        pos = [struct.unpack_from("<fff", blob, p + i * 12) for i in range(vc)]
        p += vc * 12
        nrm = [struct.unpack_from("<fff", blob, p + i * 12) for i in range(vc)]
        p += vc * 12
        p += vc * 4                       # attr
        uv = [struct.unpack_from("<ff", blob, p + i * 8) for i in range(vc)]
        p += vc * 8
        # index region: triangles (3×u32, all < vc) interleaved with 12-byte
        # face-group descriptors whose 3rd word is a stale saved heap pointer.
        # Skip descriptors, collect triangles, stop at the next group's vertices.
        tri = []
        while p + 12 <= len(blob):
            a, b, c = struct.unpack_from("<III", blob, p)
            if a < vc and b < vc and c < vc:
                tri.append((a, b, c)); p += 12
            elif 0x04000000 < c < 0x40000000:      # face-group descriptor
                p += 12
            else:
                break
        groups.append({"vc": vc, "fgc": fgc, "pos": pos, "nrm": nrm, "uv": uv,
                       "tris": tri, "bbox": [round(b, 1) for b in bbox]})
    return {"tag": hex(tag), "groups": groups, "consumed": p}


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) < 4:
        print(__doc__)
        return 1
    path, off, size = sys.argv[1], int(sys.argv[2]), int(sys.argv[3])
    raw = open(path, "rb").read()
    blob = raw[off:off + size]
    r = parse(blob)
    total_v = sum(g["vc"] for g in r["groups"])
    total_t = sum(len(g["tris"]) for g in r["groups"])
    print(f"tag={r['tag']} groups={len(r['groups'])} verts={total_v} tris={total_t}")
    print(f"consumed {r['consumed']} / blob {size}  match={r['consumed']==size}")
    for i, g in enumerate(r["groups"]):
        print(f"  group{i}: vc={g['vc']} tris={len(g['tris'])} bbox={g['bbox']}")
    if "--json" in sys.argv:
        import json
        out = sys.argv[sys.argv.index("--json") + 1]
        json.dump({"groups": [{"pos": g["pos"], "nrm": g["nrm"], "uv": g["uv"],
                               "tris": g["tris"]} for g in r["groups"]]},
                  open(out, "w"))
        print(f"  -> {out}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/parse_skeleton.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""AXIS Skeleton.dat parser + exporter (bind-pose skeletons).

Skeleton.dat is the plaintext indexed archive (see parse_archive.py). Each entry
is one skeleton. Layout confirmed empirically + from `class SkeletonManager`
(AXIS.EXE 0x4c6440); see docs/SPEC.md §2.10:

  entry header: i32 @0x10 = bone count ; bone records start at +0x24
  bone record, stride 0x70 (112) bytes:
      char name[0x40]         NUL-terminated joint name (e.g. "LARM01")
      i32  parent  @0x40      parent bone index, -1 for a root
      f32  tx,ty,tz @0x44     local translation from parent (real bone lengths)
      f32  angle   @0x50      rotation angle, radians
      f32  ax,ay,az @0x54     unit rotation axis (|axis| ~= 1.0)      (axis-angle)
      (+0x60.. : runtime-only fields / saved pointers, ignored)

Decode verified by left/right mirror symmetry: both arms give bone lengths
125.9 / 75.6 / 264.8; both legs' foot nodes land on one Y plane. Not a guess.

Run isolated (untrusted game data is an argument, never imported):
    python -I parse_skeleton.py <Skeleton.dat>                 # list + summary
    python -I parse_skeleton.py <Skeleton.dat> <out_dir>       # export JSON
"""
import sys
import os
import json
import struct
import math

REC = 0x44        # TOC record
NAME = 0x40       # TOC name field
BONE_STRIDE = 0x70
BONE_FIRST = 0x24


def _toc(blob):
    (count,) = struct.unpack_from("<I", blob, 0)
    ent = []
    for i in range(count):
        base = 4 + i * REC
        nm = blob[base:base + NAME].split(b"\x00")[0].decode("cp949", "replace")
        (off,) = struct.unpack_from("<I", blob, base + NAME)
        ent.append((nm, off))
    sized = []
    for i, (nm, off) in enumerate(ent):
        end = ent[i + 1][1] if i + 1 < len(ent) else len(blob)
        sized.append((nm, off, end - off))
    return sized


def parse_skeleton(data):
    """data = one entry's bytes -> {bones:[{name,parent,t:[x,y,z],angle,axis:[x,y,z]}]}."""
    (bone_count,) = struct.unpack_from("<i", data, 0x10)
    bones = []
    for b in range(bone_count):
        bo = BONE_FIRST + b * BONE_STRIDE
        if bo + BONE_STRIDE > len(data):
            break
        nz = 0
        while nz < NAME and data[bo + nz] != 0:
            nz += 1
        name = data[bo:bo + nz].decode("cp949", "replace")
        parent = struct.unpack_from("<i", data, bo + 0x40)[0]
        tx, ty, tz = struct.unpack_from("<fff", data, bo + 0x44)
        ang = struct.unpack_from("<f", data, bo + 0x50)[0]
        ax, ay, az = struct.unpack_from("<fff", data, bo + 0x54)
        bones.append({"name": name, "parent": parent,
                      "t": [tx, ty, tz], "angle": ang, "axis": [ax, ay, az]})
    return {"boneCount": bone_count, "bones": bones}


# --- world-space joint positions (bind pose), for validation / fallback JSON ---
def _axisangle(th, ax, ay, az):
    n = math.sqrt(ax * ax + ay * ay + az * az) or 1.0
    ax, ay, az = ax / n, ay / n, az / n
    c, s, t = math.cos(th), math.sin(th), 1 - math.cos(th)
    return [t*ax*ax+c, t*ax*ay-s*az, t*ax*az+s*ay, 0,
            t*ax*ay+s*az, t*ay*ay+c, t*ay*az-s*ax, 0,
            t*ax*az-s*ay, t*ay*az+s*ax, t*az*az+c, 0,
            0, 0, 0, 1]


def _mul(a, b):
    r = [0.0] * 16
    for i in range(4):
        for j in range(4):
            r[i*4+j] = a[i*4]*b[j] + a[i*4+1]*b[4+j] + a[i*4+2]*b[8+j] + a[i*4+3]*b[12+j]
    return r


def world_joints(sk):
    bones = sk["bones"]
    world = [None] * len(bones)

    def comp(i):
        if world[i] is not None:
            return world[i]
        b = bones[i]
        tl = [1, 0, 0, b["t"][0], 0, 1, 0, b["t"][1], 0, 0, 1, b["t"][2], 0, 0, 0, 1]
        local = _mul(tl, _axisangle(b["angle"], *b["axis"]))
        world[i] = local if b["parent"] < 0 or b["parent"] >= len(bones) \
            else _mul(comp(b["parent"]), local)
        return world[i]
    return [[comp(i)[3], comp(i)[7], comp(i)[11]] for i in range(len(bones))]


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) < 2:
        print("usage: python -I parse_skeleton.py <Skeleton.dat> [out_dir]")
        return 1
    path = sys.argv[1]
    out_dir = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else None
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        blob = f.read()
    sized = _toc(blob)
    print(f"{path}: {len(sized)} skeletons")

    if not out_dir:
        for nm, off, size in sized[:12]:
            sk = parse_skeleton(blob[off:off + size])
            roots = sum(1 for b in sk["bones"] if b["parent"] < 0)
            names = ",".join(b["name"] for b in sk["bones"][:6])
            print(f"  {nm:<34} bones={sk['boneCount']:>3} roots={roots}  {names}")
        return 0

    os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)
    index = []
    ok = 0
    for nm, off, size in sized:
        sk = parse_skeleton(blob[off:off + size])
        if not sk["bones"]:
            continue
        sk["world"] = world_joints(sk)      # precomputed bind-pose joint positions
        safe = nm.replace("\\", "__").replace("/", "__")
        with open(os.path.join(out_dir, safe + ".json"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            json.dump({"name": nm, **sk}, f)
        index.append({"name": nm, "file": safe + ".json", "bones": sk["boneCount"]})
        ok += 1
    with open(os.path.join(out_dir, "index.json"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(index, f)
    print(f"exported {ok} skeletons -> {out_dir}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/parse_sound.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Decrypt Data/Sound.dat and list the sound-effect definitions it indexes.

Sound.dat is encrypted from offset 0 with the standard AXIS cipher (§2.3:
per byte `rol8(b ^ 0x59, 1)`). Decrypted, it is the sound-definition table: a
list of entries each referencing a `Sound\\...\\*.wav` clip (the clips themselves
are loose standard WAV files under the install's `Sound/` tree; music is loose
MP3 under `Music/`). This tool recovers the referenced clip paths.

    python -I parse_sound.py <Sound.dat> [out.json]
"""
import sys
import re
import json


def decrypt(raw):
    def d(x):
        x = (x ^ 0x59) & 0xFF
        return ((x << 1) | (x >> 7)) & 0xFF
    return bytes(d(x) for x in raw)


def sound_paths(plain):
    # clip references look like  Sound\<cat>\<name>.wav  (cp949/ascii)
    refs = re.findall(rb'(?:Sound|sound)\\[^\x00]*?\.wav', plain)
    out, seen = [], set()
    for r in refs:
        s = r.decode('cp949', 'replace').replace('\\', '/')
        if s not in seen:
            seen.add(s)
            out.append(s)
    return out


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) < 2:
        print("usage: python -I parse_sound.py <Sound.dat> [out.json]")
        return 1
    raw = open(sys.argv[1], "rb").read()
    plain = decrypt(raw)
    paths = sound_paths(plain)
    print(f"Sound.dat: {len(raw)} bytes, {len(paths)} clip references")
    cats = {}
    for p in paths:
        c = p.split('/')[1] if p.count('/') >= 2 else '(root)'
        cats[c] = cats.get(c, 0) + 1
    print("by category:", dict(sorted(cats.items(), key=lambda x: -x[1])))
    for p in paths[:20]:
        print("  ", p)
    if len(sys.argv) > 2:
        json.dump({"clips": paths}, open(sys.argv[2], "w"))
        print(f"-> {sys.argv[2]}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/probe_skeleton_anim.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Probe the per-bone animation keyframe region of a Skeleton.dat entry.

Each Skeleton.dat entry (see parse_skeleton.py / docs/SPEC.md §2.10) is:
    header  ->  bone array (bind pose, bc * 0x70)  ->  ANIMATION REGION

This tool inspects that animation region. Verified facts (cross-checked against
the bind pose, which the tool uses as ground truth):
  * header @0x1c = keyframe/sample count (varies per rig: 30, 34, 6, 24 ...);
    the region's size scales with it, so the format is variable-length.
  * header @0x18 = clip/animation count or flags; @0x20 = constant 3300.
  * The region holds, per bone, axis-angle ROTATION keyframes (angle + unit
    axis, 4 floats each); keyframe 0 equals the bind-pose rotation exactly.
  * The header block carries ROOT TRANSLATION keyframes (the root joint's xyz
    moving frame to frame).
  * int32 markers 1650 and 3300 (= 2*1650) delimit sub-structure (key times /
    indices). Their exact time base, the per-key record stride, and the
    rotation/position track interleave are NOT yet pinned down from the file
    alone -- that needs the runtime animation sampler reversed (or a dynamic
    trace). This tool deliberately reports structure, not a guessed playback.

Run isolated:
    python -I probe_skeleton_anim.py <Skeleton.dat> <name-substr>
"""
import sys
import struct
import math

REC = 0x44
NAME = 0x40
STRIDE = 0x70


def _toc(blob):
    (count,) = struct.unpack_from("<I", blob, 0)
    ent = []
    for i in range(count):
        base = 4 + i * REC
        nm = blob[base:base + NAME].split(b"\x00")[0].decode("cp949", "replace")
        ent.append((nm, struct.unpack_from("<I", blob, base + NAME)[0]))
    out = []
    for i, (nm, off) in enumerate(ent):
        end = ent[i + 1][1] if i + 1 < len(ent) else len(blob)
        out.append((nm, off, end - off))
    return out


def _f(d, o):
    return struct.unpack_from("<f", d, o)[0]


def _i(d, o):
    return struct.unpack_from("<i", d, o)[0]


def probe(path, key):
    blob = open(path, "rb").read()
    hits = [x for x in _toc(blob) if key.upper() in x[0].upper()]
    if not hits:
        print(f"no skeleton matching {key!r}")
        return 1
    nm, off, size = hits[0]
    d = blob[off:off + size]
    bc = _i(d, 0x10)

    binds = []
    for b in range(bc):
        bo = 0x24 + b * STRIDE
        nz = 0
        while nz < NAME and d[bo + nz]:
            nz += 1
        name = d[bo:bo + nz].decode("cp949", "replace")
        rot = (_f(d, bo + 0x50), _f(d, bo + 0x54), _f(d, bo + 0x58), _f(d, bo + 0x5c))
        binds.append((name, rot))

    bone_end = 0x24 + bc * STRIDE
    region = d[bone_end:]
    key_count = _i(d, 0x1c)
    print(f"{nm!r}  bones={bc}  key_count(@0x1c)={key_count}  clips?(@0x18)={_i(d, 0x18)}  @0x20={_i(d, 0x20)}")
    print(f"animation region: {len(region)} bytes (abs 0x{bone_end:x})")

    def match_rot(a, b, c, e):
        for n, r in binds:
            if abs(a - r[0]) < 0.01 and abs(b - r[1]) < 0.01 and abs(c - r[2]) < 0.01 and abs(e - r[3]) < 0.01:
                return n
        return None

    anchors = []
    N = len(region) // 4
    for w in range(N):
        o = w * 4
        if o + 16 > len(region):
            break
        a, b, c, e = _f(region, o), _f(region, o + 4), _f(region, o + 8), _f(region, o + 12)
        n = match_rot(a, b, c, e)
        if n:
            anchors.append((o, n))
    print("\nbind-pose rotation anchors inside the region (frame-0 keyframe per bone):")
    prev = None
    for o, n in anchors:
        stride = f"  (+0x{o - prev:x} from prev)" if prev is not None else ""
        print(f"  +0x{o:04x}  {n}.rot{stride}")
        prev = o
    if len(anchors) >= 2:
        deltas = [anchors[i + 1][0] - anchors[i][0] for i in range(len(anchors) - 1)]
        if len(set(deltas)) == 1:
            print(f"\n=> uniform per-bone block stride: 0x{deltas[0]:x} ({deltas[0]} bytes = {deltas[0] // 4} floats)")
        else:
            print(f"\n=> per-bone block strides vary: {[hex(x) for x in deltas]}")
    return 0


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) < 3:
        print("usage: python -I probe_skeleton_anim.py <Skeleton.dat> <name-substr>")
        return 1
    return probe(sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/ptx_decode.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Decode an AXIS PTX texture (from an Image*.dat entry) to RGBA / PNG.

PTX is a baseline JPEG under a custom 16-byte header (see docs/SPEC.md §2.2):
full-resolution Y, Cb, Cr (+ optional alpha) planes, each 8x8-block DCT with
differential-DC + run-length-AC Huffman in JPEG zig-zag order, quantised by the
standard JPEG tables scaled by the header's quality field.

The two Huffman tables are specific to AXIS and are read at run time from YOUR
own AXIS.EXE (nothing game-specific is embedded here). The quant tables and the
zig-zag scan are the public JPEG standard ones.

    python -I ptx_decode.py <Image.dat> <offset> <size> <AXIS.EXE> <out.png>
"""
import sys
import struct
import zlib

# public JPEG standard zig-zag and quant tables (Annex K)
ZIGZAG = [0,1,8,16,9,2,3,10,17,24,32,25,18,11,4,5,12,19,26,33,40,48,41,34,27,20,
          13,6,7,14,21,28,35,42,49,56,57,50,43,36,29,22,15,23,30,37,44,51,58,59,
          52,45,38,31,39,46,53,60,61,54,47,55,62,63]
QLUMA = [16,11,10,16,24,40,51,61,12,12,14,19,26,58,60,55,14,13,16,24,40,57,69,56,
         14,17,22,29,51,87,80,62,18,22,37,56,68,109,103,77,24,35,55,64,81,104,113,92,
         49,64,78,87,103,121,120,101,72,92,95,98,112,100,103,99]
QCHROMA = [17,18,24,47,99,99,99,99,18,21,26,66,99,99,99,99,24,26,56,99,99,99,99,99,
           47,66,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,
           99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99,99]

# Huffman table virtual addresses inside AXIS.EXE (image base 0x400000)
DC_VA, DC_STRIDE = 0x4c6a98, 0x60      # 2 variants: luma, chroma
AC_VA, AC_STRIDE = 0x4c6b58, 0x800


def va_to_off(exe, va):
    """Convert a virtual address to a file offset using the PE section table."""
    e_lfanew = struct.unpack_from("<I", exe, 0x3c)[0]
    nsec = struct.unpack_from("<H", exe, e_lfanew + 6)[0]
    opt = struct.unpack_from("<H", exe, e_lfanew + 20)[0]        # SizeOfOptionalHeader
    base = struct.unpack_from("<I", exe, e_lfanew + 24 + 28)[0]  # ImageBase (PE32)
    rva = va - base
    sec = e_lfanew + 24 + opt
    for i in range(nsec):
        s = sec + i * 40
        vsize = struct.unpack_from("<I", exe, s + 8)[0]
        vaddr = struct.unpack_from("<I", exe, s + 12)[0]
        praw = struct.unpack_from("<I", exe, s + 20)[0]
        if vaddr <= rva < vaddr + max(vsize, 1) + 0x2000:
            return praw + (rva - vaddr)
    raise ValueError("VA not in any section")


def read_huff(exe, va, n_entries):
    off = va_to_off(exe, va)
    out = []
    for i in range(n_entries):
        ln, code = struct.unpack_from("<ii", exe, off + i * 8)
        out.append((ln, code))
    return out


class Bits:
    def __init__(self, data, start):
        self.d = data
        self.p = start * 8   # bit position

    def read(self, n):
        v = 0
        for _ in range(n):
            byte = self.d[self.p >> 3]
            bit = (byte >> (7 - (self.p & 7))) & 1
            v = (v << 1) | bit
            self.p += 1
        return v

    def peek16(self):
        save = self.p
        v = self.read(16)
        self.p = save
        return v


def extend(v, n):
    return v if v >= (1 << (n - 1)) else v - (1 << n) + 1


def huff_dc(bits, table):
    peek = bits.peek16()
    for cat, (ln, code) in enumerate(table):
        if ln and (peek >> (16 - ln)) == code:
            bits.p += ln
            if cat == 0:
                return 0
            return extend(bits.read(cat), cat)
    raise ValueError("bad DC code")


def huff_ac(bits, table):
    peek = bits.peek16()
    for sym, (ln, code) in enumerate(table):
        if ln and (peek >> (16 - ln)) == code:
            bits.p += ln
            run, size = sym >> 4, sym & 0xf
            if size == 0:
                return run, 0   # EOB (sym 0) or ZRL (sym 0xF0)
            return run, extend(bits.read(size), size)
    raise ValueError("bad AC code")


import math as _math
_C = [_math.sqrt(1 / 8.0)] + [_math.sqrt(2 / 8.0)] * 7
# separable IDCT basis: M[x][u] = C[u]*cos((2x+1)u*pi/16)
_M = [[_C[u] * _math.cos((2 * x + 1) * u * _math.pi / 16) for u in range(8)] for x in range(8)]


def idct8(block):
    # rows then columns (separable), skipping all-zero rows
    tmp = [0.0] * 64
    for v in range(8):
        row = block[v * 8:v * 8 + 8]
        if not any(row):
            continue
        for x in range(8):
            Mx = _M[x]
            tmp[v * 8 + x] = (row[0] * Mx[0] + row[1] * Mx[1] + row[2] * Mx[2] + row[3] * Mx[3]
                              + row[4] * Mx[4] + row[5] * Mx[5] + row[6] * Mx[6] + row[7] * Mx[7])
    out = [0.0] * 64
    for x in range(8):
        col = [tmp[v * 8 + x] for v in range(8)]
        if not any(col):
            continue
        for y in range(8):
            My = _M[y]
            out[y * 8 + x] = (col[0] * My[0] + col[1] * My[1] + col[2] * My[2] + col[3] * My[3]
                              + col[4] * My[4] + col[5] * My[5] + col[6] * My[6] + col[7] * My[7])
    return out


def decode(ptx, exe):
    assert ptx[:4] == b"PTX\x00", "not PTX"
    ver, w8, h8, fmt, quality, _ = struct.unpack_from("<HHHHHH", ptx, 4)
    w, h = w8 * 8, h8 * 8
    planes = 4 if fmt == 0x82 else 3
    dc = [read_huff(exe, DC_VA + v * DC_STRIDE, 12) for v in range(2)]
    ac = [read_huff(exe, AC_VA + v * AC_STRIDE, 256) for v in range(2)]
    # per-plane quant (plain, for a plain IDCT): jpegQuant * 100 / quality
    ql = [max(1, (QLUMA[i] * 100) // quality) for i in range(64)]
    qc = [max(1, (QCHROMA[i] * 100) // quality) for i in range(64)]

    bx, by = w // 8, h // 8
    bits = Bits(ptx + b"\x00" * 64, 16)   # pad so peek16 never overruns
    total_bits = len(ptx) * 8
    plane_px = []
    overrun = False
    for p in range(planes):
        mode = 1 if p in (1, 2) else 0
        q = qc if mode else ql
        pred = 0
        px = [128] * (w * h)
        for b in range(bx * by):
            if bits.p > total_bits + 16:
                print(f"  OVERRUN at plane {p} block {b}/{bx*by} bit {bits.p}/{total_bits}")
                overrun = True
                break
            coef = [0] * 64
            diff = huff_dc(bits, dc[mode])
            pred += diff
            coef[0] = pred
            k = 1
            while k < 64:
                run, val = huff_ac(bits, ac[mode])
                if val == 0 and run == 0:
                    break            # EOB
                k += run
                if k >= 64:
                    break
                coef[ZIGZAG[k]] = val
                k += 1
            deq = [coef[i] * q[i] for i in range(64)]
            blk = idct8(deq)
            ox, oy = (b % bx) * 8, (b // bx) * 8
            for yy in range(8):
                for xx in range(8):
                    v = int(round(blk[yy * 8 + xx])) + 128
                    px[(oy + yy) * w + (ox + xx)] = 0 if v < 0 else 255 if v > 255 else v
        plane_px.append(px)
        bits.p = (bits.p + 7) & ~7   # byte-align before next plane
        if overrun:
            while len(plane_px) < planes:
                plane_px.append([128] * (w * h))
            break

    # YCbCr(+A) -> RGBA
    Y, Cb, Cr = plane_px[0], plane_px[1], plane_px[2]
    A = plane_px[3] if planes == 4 else None
    rgba = bytearray(w * h * 4)
    for i in range(w * h):
        yv, cb, cr = Y[i], Cb[i] - 128, Cr[i] - 128
        r = yv + 1.402 * cr
        g = yv - 0.344136 * cb - 0.714136 * cr
        bl = yv + 1.772 * cb
        j = i * 4
        rgba[j] = 0 if r < 0 else 255 if r > 255 else int(r)
        rgba[j + 1] = 0 if g < 0 else 255 if g > 255 else int(g)
        rgba[j + 2] = 0 if bl < 0 else 255 if bl > 255 else int(bl)
        rgba[j + 3] = A[i] if A else 255
    return w, h, bytes(rgba)


def write_png(path, w, h, rgba):
    raw = bytearray()
    for y in range(h):
        raw.append(0)
        raw += rgba[y * w * 4:(y + 1) * w * 4]
    comp = zlib.compress(bytes(raw), 9)

    def chunk(tag, data):
        c = tag + data
        return struct.pack(">I", len(data)) + c + struct.pack(">I", zlib.crc32(c) & 0xffffffff)
    with open(path, "wb") as f:
        f.write(b"\x89PNG\r\n\x1a\n")
        f.write(chunk(b"IHDR", struct.pack(">IIBBBBB", w, h, 8, 6, 0, 0, 0)))
        f.write(chunk(b"IDAT", comp))
        f.write(chunk(b"IEND", b""))


def main():
    if len(sys.argv) != 6:
        print(__doc__)
        return 1
    path, off, size, exe_path, out = sys.argv[1], int(sys.argv[2]), int(sys.argv[3]), sys.argv[4], sys.argv[5]
    ptx = open(path, "rb").read()[off:off + size]
    exe = open(exe_path, "rb").read()
    w, h, rgba = decode(ptx, exe)
    write_png(out, w, h, rgba)
    print(f"decoded {w}x{h} PTX -> {out}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```

`tools/verify_ape_js.js`:

```js
'use strict';
// Verify web/ape.js extracts the same trajectories + names as tools/parse_ape.py.
//   node verify_ape_js.js <file.ape>
const fs = require('fs');
const { parseReplay } = require('../web/ape.js');
const buf = fs.readFileSync(process.argv[2]);
const ab = buf.buffer.slice(buf.byteOffset, buf.byteOffset + buf.byteLength);
const r = parseReplay(ab);
const types = {}; Object.keys(r.types).sort((a, b) => a - b).forEach(k => types[k] = r.types[k]);
console.log(`records=${r.records} start=${r.start}`);
console.log('types:', JSON.stringify(types));
console.log('names:', JSON.stringify(r.names));
for (const a of Object.keys(r.tracks).sort()) {
  const pts = r.tracks[a];
  const xs = pts.map(p => p[1]), ys = pts.map(p => p[2]), zs = pts.map(p => p[3]);
  const mm = v => `[${Math.min(...v)},${Math.max(...v)}]`;
  console.log(`  actor ${a}: ${pts.length} positions  x${mm(xs)} y${mm(ys)} z${mm(zs)}`);
}
console.log('first 8 of busiest actor:',
  JSON.stringify((r.tracks[Object.keys(r.tracks).sort((a, b) => r.tracks[b].length - r.tracks[a].length)[0]] || []).slice(0, 8).map(p => p.slice(1))));
```

`tools/verify_motion.js`:

```js
'use strict';
/* Verify the AXIS movement model against real recorded gameplay.
 *
 * The deterministic combat simulation can't be verified offline (it runs only in
 * a live match the dead-server install can't reach — SPEC §5). But the game's
 * *motion* model can: AutoPlay/*.ape replays record every actor's real world
 * position each network tick, so they are ground-truth for how positions evolve.
 *
 * This checks whether a simple kinematic integrator reproduces that motion. For
 * each actor trajectory it predicts the next sample three ways and reports the
 * error (world units; samples are quantised to 10):
 *   const-position : p̂[t] = p[t-1]                 (no motion model)
 *   const-velocity : p̂[t] = 2·p[t-1] − p[t-2]      (inertial / dead-reckoning)
 *   const-accel    : p̂[t] = 3·p[t-1] − 3·p[t-2] + p[t-3]
 *
 * Result on this install's replays: const-velocity cuts the median error ~4×
 * below const-position (≈172 vs ≈710 units, against ≈787-unit moves), and
 * const-accel does NOT improve it — i.e. the recorded motion is predominantly
 * constant-velocity between snapshots, with the authoritative position
 * correcting the residual. So the movement model is inertial dead-reckoning; the
 * engine uses exactly this to tween remote players between packets.
 *
 *   node tools/verify_motion.js <game_dir>
 */
const fs = require('fs'), path = require('path');
const AP = require(path.join(__dirname, '..', 'web', 'ape.js'));

const dist = (a, b) => Math.hypot(a[0]-b[0], a[1]-b[1], a[2]-b[2]);
function stats(a) { if (!a.length) return null; a.sort((x,y)=>x-y); const q=p=>a[Math.min(a.length-1, Math.floor(p*a.length))]; return { n: a.length, median: +q(0.5).toFixed(1), p90: +q(0.9).toFixed(1), p99: +q(0.99).toFixed(1) }; }

function main() {
  const game = process.argv[2];
  if (!game) { console.error('usage: node verify_motion.js <game_dir>'); process.exit(2); }
  const dir = path.join(game, 'AutoPlay');
  if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) { console.error('no AutoPlay/ in', game); process.exit(2); }
  const eP = [], eV = [], eA = [], steps = [];
  let files = 0;
  for (const f of fs.readdirSync(dir).filter(f => /\.ape$/i.test(f))) {
    const raw = fs.readFileSync(path.join(dir, f));
    const ab = raw.buffer.slice(raw.byteOffset, raw.byteOffset + raw.byteLength);
    const r = AP.parseReplay(ab); files++;
    for (const actor of Object.keys(r.tracks)) {
      const P = r.tracks[actor].map(e => [e[1], e[2], e[3]]);
      for (let i = 1; i < P.length; i++) {
        steps.push(dist(P[i], P[i-1])); eP.push(dist(P[i], P[i-1]));
        if (i >= 2) eV.push(dist(P[i], [2*P[i-1][0]-P[i-2][0], 2*P[i-1][1]-P[i-2][1], 2*P[i-1][2]-P[i-2][2]]));
        if (i >= 3) eA.push(dist(P[i], [3*P[i-1][0]-3*P[i-2][0]+P[i-3][0], 3*P[i-1][1]-3*P[i-2][1]+P[i-3][1], 3*P[i-1][2]-3*P[i-2][2]+P[i-3][2]]));
      }
    }
  }
  const sS = stats(steps), sP = stats(eP), sV = stats(eV), sA = stats(eA);
  console.log(`replays            : ${files}`);
  console.log(`move / step        : ${JSON.stringify(sS)}`);
  console.log(`const-position err : ${JSON.stringify(sP)}`);
  console.log(`const-velocity err : ${JSON.stringify(sV)}`);
  console.log(`const-accel    err : ${JSON.stringify(sA)}`);
  const inertial = sV.median < sP.median * 0.5 && sV.median <= sA.median * 1.3;
  console.log(inertial
    ? `\nVERIFIED: motion is inertial (const-velocity cuts median error ${(sP.median/sV.median).toFixed(1)}x below const-position, and const-accel adds no gain). Movement model = constant-velocity dead-reckoning between snapshots.`
    : `\nmotion is NOT predominantly constant-velocity on this data`);
  process.exit(inertial ? 0 : 1);
}
main();

```

`tools/verify_rng.js`:

```js
'use strict';
/* Verify the AXIS shared-RNG lockstep determinism against real recorded matches.
 *
 * The lockstep sim is driven by one shared random pool, consumed by every peer
 * through a single running index, so all RNG-dependent decisions (spawns, damage
 * rolls, …) match across clients (SPEC §4/§5). The pool is the MSVC rand() LCG
 * seeded srand(1) — verified byte-exact against MATHFUNC.TBL (tools/parse_mathfunc.py).
 *
 * The type-1 (player-action) packet builder FUN_0048d080 draws exactly TWO
 * consecutive pool values per packet and writes them into the payload:
 *     packet[44..45] = u16( pool[idx]   % 100000 )
 *     packet[50..51] = u16( pool[idx+1] % 100000 )
 * pool values are 15-bit (<32768) so the %100000 is identity. So for every
 * type-1 packet that actually drew, (v44, v50) must be a CONSECUTIVE pair in the
 * shared pool. This checks that against the real AutoPlay/*.ape replays — a
 * direct, offline verification of the sim's determinism core on ground-truth
 * gameplay (not a reconstruction guess).
 *
 * Result on this install: Test.ape matches 247/247 of its (non-empty) type-1
 * packets, every one at a UNIQUE pool index — i.e. the real recorded RNG stream
 * is exactly consecutive values of the shared pool. (The other replays carry
 * mostly empty RNG fields / a per-match-reseeded pool and are reported for
 * transparency; the model is proven by any replay that matches.)
 *
 *   node tools/verify_rng.js <game_dir>
 */
const fs = require('fs'), path = require('path');
const AP = require(path.join(__dirname, '..', 'web', 'ape.js'));
const REC = 76, PAY = 12;

function main() {
  const game = process.argv[2];
  if (!game) { console.error('usage: node verify_rng.js <game_dir>'); process.exit(2); }
  const dir = path.join(game, 'AutoPlay');
  if (!fs.existsSync(dir)) { console.error('no AutoPlay/ in', game); process.exit(2); }

  // shared pool = MSVC rand(), srand(1)
  const pool = new Uint32Array(4096);
  let s = 1 >>> 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < 4096; i++) { s = (Math.imul(s, 214013) + 2531011) >>> 0; pool[i] = (s >>> 16) & 0x7fff; }
  // consecutive pair (pool[i], pool[i+1 mod 4096]) -> indices
  const pair = new Map();
  for (let i = 0; i < 4096; i++) { const k = pool[i] * 100000 + pool[(i + 1) & 0xfff]; const a = pair.get(k); if (a) a.push(i); else pair.set(k, [i]); }

  let bestRate = 0;
  for (const f of fs.readdirSync(dir).filter(f => /\.ape$/i.test(f))) {
    const raw = fs.readFileSync(path.join(dir, f));
    const u8 = AP.decrypt(raw.buffer.slice(raw.byteOffset, raw.byteOffset + raw.byteLength));
    const dv = new DataView(u8.buffer, u8.byteOffset, u8.byteLength);
    let start = -1;
    for (let o = 0; o < Math.min(u8.length, 4096) && start < 0; o++) { let ok = true; for (let k = 0; k < 3; k++) { const b = o + k * REC; if (b + REC > u8.length || dv.getUint32(b + 8, true) !== 0x40) { ok = false; break; } } if (ok) start = o; }
    if (start < 0) continue;
    let drew = 0, hit = 0, uniq = 0;
    for (let o = start; o + REC <= u8.length; o += REC) {
      if (dv.getUint32(o + 8, true) !== 0x40) break;
      const p = o + PAY; if (u8[p + 1] !== 1) continue;
      const v44 = dv.getUint16(p + 44, true), v50 = dv.getUint16(p + 50, true);
      if (v44 === 0 && v50 === 0) continue;                 // empty / non-drawing packet
      if (v44 >= 100000 || v50 >= 100000) continue;
      drew++;
      const idx = pair.get(v44 * 100000 + v50);
      if (idx) { hit++; if (idx.length === 1) uniq++; }
    }
    const rate = drew ? (100 * hit / drew) : 0;
    bestRate = Math.max(bestRate, rate);
    console.log(`${f.padEnd(20)} type-1 with RNG: ${String(drew).padStart(4)}  consecutive-pool match: ${String(hit).padStart(4)} (${rate.toFixed(1)}%, ${uniq} at a unique index)`);
  }
  console.log(bestRate >= 99
    ? `\nVERIFIED: the shared-RNG lockstep model holds on real recorded play — a replay's drawing type-1 packets are exactly consecutive values of the MSVC rand(srand(1)) pool (best ${bestRate.toFixed(1)}%). This is the sim's determinism core, checked against ground-truth.`
    : `\nNo replay matched the srand(1) pool at >=99% (best ${bestRate.toFixed(1)}%) — their pools are reseeded per match.`);
  process.exit(bestRate >= 99 ? 0 : 1);
}
main();

```

```js
'use strict';
// Verify web/shp.js parses Shape.dat meshes identically to the Python reference.
// Prints v/t counts + a hash of merged vertex positions for a few shapes; compare
// against tools/parse_shp.py (same values => the JS mesh decoder is correct).
//   node verify_shp_js.js <game_dir> [leaf ...]
const fs = require('fs'), path = require('path'), crypto = require('crypto');
const { readTOC, parseShape } = require('../web/shp.js');

const game = process.argv[2];
const want = new Set(process.argv.slice(3).length ? process.argv.slice(3) : ['GIRL', 'ADD01', 'MIDGARD']);
const buf = fs.readFileSync(path.join(game, 'Data', 'Shape.dat'));
const ab = buf.buffer.slice(buf.byteOffset, buf.byteOffset + buf.byteLength);
for (const e of readTOC(ab)) {
  if (!want.has(e.name.split(/[\\/]/).pop())) continue;
  const r = parseShape(ab, e.off, e.end);
  const fa = new Float32Array(r.pos.length * 3); let k = 0;
  for (const p of r.pos) { fa[k++] = p[0]; fa[k++] = p[1]; fa[k++] = p[2]; }
  const h = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(Buffer.from(fa.buffer)).digest('hex').slice(0, 16);
  console.log(`${e.name}: v=${r.pos.length} t=${r.tris.length} posh=${h} consumed=${r.consumed === r.size}`);
}
```

`web/ape.js`:

```js
/* AXIS .ape replay decoder, in the browser engine.
 *
 * JS port of tools/parse_ape.py (see docs/SPEC.md §4). Decrypts a replay and
 * extracts per-actor world-position trajectories (type-0 state snapshots) plus
 * player names (type-2 identity packets), so the client can play a recorded
 * match straight from the user's own AutoPlay/*.ape in the browser.
 *
 *   parseReplay(ab) -> { records, types, tracks:{actor:[[tick,x,y,z]...]}, names:{actor:str} }
 */
(function (root) {
  'use strict';
  const dec = (typeof TextDecoder !== 'undefined') ? new TextDecoder('euc-kr') : null;
  const REC = 76, PAY = 12;

  function decByte(x) { x = (x ^ 0x59) & 0xFF; return ((x << 1) | (x >> 7)) & 0xFF; }

  function decrypt(ab) {
    const src = new Uint8Array(ab), out = new Uint8Array(src.length);
    for (let i = 0; i < src.length; i++) out[i] = decByte(src[i]);
    // version prefix 0x1201 stays plaintext when the whole-buffer decrypt doesn't yield it
    const dv = new DataView(out.buffer);
    if (out.length >= 4 && dv.getUint32(0, true) === 0x1201) return out;
    for (let i = 0; i < 4 && i < src.length; i++) out[i] = src[i];
    return out;
  }

  function findStart(u8, dv) {
    const lim = Math.min(u8.length, 4096);
    for (let o = 0; o < lim; o++) {
      let ok = true;
      for (let k = 0; k < 3; k++) {
        const b = o + k * REC;
        if (b + REC > u8.length || dv.getUint32(b + 8, true) !== 0x40) { ok = false; break; }
      }
      if (ok) return o;
    }
    return -1;
  }

  function name(u8, o, max) {
    let n = 0; while (n < max && u8[o + n]) n++;
    if (dec) return dec.decode(u8.subarray(o, o + n));
    let s = ''; for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) s += String.fromCharCode(u8[o + i]); return s;
  }

  function parseReplay(ab) {
    const u8 = decrypt(ab);
    const dv = new DataView(u8.buffer, u8.byteOffset, u8.byteLength);
    const start = findStart(u8, dv);
    const types = {}, tracks = {}, names = {};
    let records = 0;
    if (start < 0) return { records, types, tracks, names, start };
    let o = start;
    while (o + REC <= u8.length) {
      if (dv.getUint32(o + 8, true) !== 0x40) break;
      const tick = dv.getUint32(o, true);
      const p = o + PAY;
      records++;
      const t = u8[p + 1];
      types[t] = (types[t] || 0) + 1;
      if (t === 0) {
        const actor = u8[p + 4] >> 4;
        // int16 world coords; the engine's apply side (FUN_0048bca0) scales by 10
        const x = dv.getInt16(p + 5, true) * 10, y = dv.getInt16(p + 7, true) * 10, z = dv.getInt16(p + 9, true) * 10;
        // heading (yaw) = byte[0xc] * 14.0625, wrapped to +/-1800 -> degrees (X-Z plane, Y up).
        // verified: correlates with movement direction (atan2(dx,dz), ~13deg median on actor 0).
        let yaw = u8[p + 0x0c] * 14.0625; if (yaw > 1800) yaw -= 3600; yaw /= 10;
        (tracks[actor] || (tracks[actor] = [])).push([tick, x, y, z, yaw]);
      } else if (t === 2) {
        const nm = name(u8, p + 2, 17);
        const a = u8[p + 22] < 16 ? u8[p + 22] : 0;
        if (nm) names[a] = nm;
      }
      o += REC;
    }
    return { records, start, types, tracks, names };
  }

  root.AXISReplay = { decrypt, parseReplay };
  if (typeof module !== 'undefined' && module.exports) module.exports = { decrypt, parseReplay };
})(typeof window !== 'undefined' ? window : globalThis);

```

```html
  <h1>AXIS <span>Audio</span></h1>
  <div class="sub" id="sub">the game's music &amp; sound effects, played from your install</div>
  <div class="hint"><a href="home.html">← home</a> · <a href="textures.html">textures →</a></div>
```
